Graham was a [neoconservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoconservative).[^cite_note-137][^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139][^cite_note-140] [Tea Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_Party_movement) opponents called Graham a "[moderate Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Factions_in_the_Republican_Party_\(United_States\)#Moderates)".[^cite_note-Raju-2014-69][^cite_note-Wagstaff-2013-141] He called himself a "[Reagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reagan)-style Republican", and was called a fairly conservative Republican with "a twang of moderation" and "an independent streak".[^cite_note-Grove-1998-8][^cite_note-BaltimoreSun-2004-15][^cite_note-Killian-2014-67]

Much of the Tea Party criticism concerned his willingness to be bipartisan and work with Democrats on issues like [climate change](5042951.md), [tax reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_reform), and [immigration reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_reform) and his belief that judicial nominees should not be opposed solely because of their philosophical positions.[^cite_note-142][^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144][^cite_note-Killian-2014-67][^cite_note-Jonsson-2014-145][^cite_note-146] He voted to confirm both of President Obama's Supreme Court nominees, [Sonia Sotomayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonia_Sotomayor) in 2009 and [Elena Kagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elena_Kagan) in 2010.[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148] Graham criticized and confronted the Tea Party, arguing for a more inclusive Republican Party.[^cite_note-Ball-2014-10][^cite_note-Draper-2010-34][^cite_note-Murphy-2014-68][^cite_note-Raju-2014-69][^cite_note-Wagstaff-2013-141][^cite_note-Jonsson-2014-145] In 2023, the Lugar Center ranked Graham in the top third of senators for bipartisanship.[^cite_note-149]

### Supreme Court nominations

In 2016, after Supreme Court Justice [Antonin Scalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonin_Scalia) died, Republican senators boycotted Obama's [nomination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrick_Garland_Supreme_Court_nomination) of [Merrick Garland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrick_Garland). Graham said that Supreme Court vacancies should never be filled in a presidential election year[^cite_note-150] and that "\[w]e are setting a precedent today, Republicans are."[^cite_note-151] He said that if a similar situation arose, "you can use my words against me and you'd be absolutely right."[^cite_note-152] In an October 2018 interview, Graham said specifically that "\[i]f an opening comes in the last year of President Trump's term, and the primary process has started, we'll wait till the next election."[^cite_note-153]

During the 2018 confirmation hearings following [Brett Kavanaugh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brett_Kavanaugh)'s nomination to the [Supreme Court of the United States](31737.md), Graham took a strong stance against letting the process be further delayed by [Christine Blasey Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_Blasey_Ford)'s allegations that Kavanaugh sexually assaulted her decades earlier when the two were in high school.[^cite_note-154] Speaking to reporters immediately after the [Senate Judiciary Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Judiciary_Committee) questioned Ford, Graham declared himself unmoved by her testimony, doubting her recollection that it was Kavanaugh who had assaulted her.[^cite_note-155]

When Kavanaugh testified before the committee the following day, Graham used his time to speak in Kavanaugh's defense, describing him as a victim who had been put through "hell" by "the most unethical sham" he had seen in his time in politics and that if Kavanaugh was looking for fair process, he had come "to the wrong town at the wrong time".[^cite_note-156]

In 2019, Graham became chair of the Judiciary Committee.[^cite_note-157] In May 2020, Graham said the Senate would work to confirm a Supreme Court nominee if a vacancy arose before the November election.[^cite_note-158] He said "Merrick Garland was a different situation. You had the president of one party nominating, and you had the Senate in the hands of the other party. A situation where you've got them both would be different."[^cite_note-159] In August 2020, he said, "After Kavanaugh's confirmation, the rules have changed as far as I'm concerned."[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-Edmondson-2020-161]

On September 18, 2020, 45 days before the presidential election, Supreme Court justice [Ruth Bader Ginsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Bader_Ginsburg) died. Within a day, Graham expressed support for the Senate immediately voting on Trump's nominee to succeed her. The *New York Times* called Graham's position "a complete and brazen reversal" of his earlier stance.[^cite_note-Edmondson-2020-161] Graham said that in 2013, years before his 2016 pledge, Democrats had changed Senate rules to allow a simple majority vote for nominees to [United States courts of appeals](70228.md).[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163]

### Free speech



Graham (far right) at the signing of the [Fair Sentencing Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fair_Sentencing_Act) in 2010

During an April 3, 2011, appearance on *[Face the Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Face_the_Nation)*,[^cite_note-164] Graham "suggested that Congress take unspecified though formal action against the [Koran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koran)-[burning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Qur'an-burning_controversy#Mock_trial_and_burning_of_a_Qur'an) by Florida preacher [Terry Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Qur'an-burning_controversy#Pastor_Terry_Jones)", in light of [an attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Mazar-i-Sharif_attack) on United Nations personnel triggered by Jones's actions.[^cite_note-Greenwald-2011-165] Asserting that "Congress might need to explore the need to limit some forms of freedom of speech",[^cite_note-166] Graham argued, "Free speech is a great idea, but we're in a war," and claimed that "during [World War II](32927.md), we had limits on what you could say if it would inspire the enemy."[^cite_note-Greenwald-2011-165][^cite_note-167]

### Gang of 14

On May 23, 2005, Graham was one of the so-called [Gang of 14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gang_of_14) senators forging a compromise that brought a halt to the continued blockage of an up-or-down vote on judicial nominees. This compromise negated both the Democrats' use of a [filibuster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filibuster) and the Republican "[nuclear option](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_option)". Under the agreement, the Democrats retained the power to filibuster a Bush judicial nominee only in an "extraordinary circumstance", and subsequently, three conservative Bush [appellate court](643.md) nominees ([Janice Rogers Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janice_Rogers_Brown), [Priscilla Owen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priscilla_Owen), and [William H. Pryor Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_H._Pryor_Jr.)) received a vote by the full Senate.[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169]

### National Security Agency surveillance

In response to the [2013 disclosures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_surveillance_disclosures_\(2013%E2%80%93present\)) about the United States [National Security Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Agency) and its international partners' [global surveillance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_surveillance) of foreign nationals and U.S. citizens, Graham said he was "glad" the NSA was collecting phone records. He said, "I'm a [Verizon](18619278.md) customer. I don't mind Verizon turning over records to the government if the government is going to make sure that they try to match up a known terrorist phone with somebody in the United States. I don't think you're talking to the terrorists. I know you're not. I know I'm not. So we don't have anything to worry about."[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171]

On July 25, 2013, the [U.S. Senate Committee on Appropriations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Senate_Committee_on_Appropriations) unanimously adopted an amendment by Graham to the Fiscal Year 2014 Department of State, Foreign Operations, and Related Programs Appropriations Bill[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] that sought sanctions against any country that offered asylum to former NSA contractor [Edward Snowden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Snowden).[^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177]

### Detainee interrogations

In July 2005, Graham secured the declassification and release of memoranda outlining concerns made by senior military lawyers as early as 2003 about the legality of the interrogations of prisoners held at [Guantanamo Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guantanamo_Bay).[^cite_note-178]

Of U.S. citizens accused of supporting terrorism, Graham said before the Senate, "When they say, 'I want my lawyer,' you tell them, 'Shut up. You don't get a lawyer. You are an enemy combatant, and we are going to talk to you about why you joined [Al Qaeda](1921.md).'"[^cite_note-179] In response to this and a June 2004 United States Supreme Court decision allowing detainees to file [habeas corpus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habeas_corpus) petitions to challenge their detentions, Graham authored an amendment to a Department of [Defense Authorization Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Authorization_Act)[^cite_note-180] attempting to clarify the authority of American courts. The amendment passed in November 2005 by a vote of 49–42 in the Senate despite opposition from [human rights](13831.md) groups and legal scholars who contended that it limited the rights of detainees.[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182]

Graham said he amended the Department of Defense Authorization Act in order to give military lawyers, as opposed to politically appointed lawyers, a more independent role in the oversight of military commanders. He argued that two of the largest problems leading to the detainee abuse scandals at Guantanamo Bay and [Abu Ghraib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Ghraib_prison) were this lack of oversight and troops' confusion over legal boundaries.[^cite_note-183]

Graham added that military lawyers had long observed the provisions of the [Uniform Code of Military Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uniform_Code_of_Military_Justice) and the [Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Convention), but that the [George W. Bush administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_W._Bush_administration) had not considered those provisions in decisions about the treatment of Guantanamo Bay detainees. He claimed that better legal oversight within the military's chain of command would prevent future detainee abuse.[^cite_note-184]

In February 2006, Graham joined Senator [Jon Kyl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Kyl) in filing an [amicus brief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amicus_brief) in the *[Hamdan v. Rumsfeld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamdan_v._Rumsfeld)* case that argued "Congress was aware" that the [Detainee Treatment Act of 2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detainee_Treatment_Act_of_2005) would strip the Supreme Court of jurisdiction to hear "pending cases, including this case" brought by Guantanamo detainees.[^cite_note-185]

In a May 2009 [CNN](62028.md) interview, Graham referred to the domestic internment of German and [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_American_internment) [prisoners of war](25008.md) and U.S. citizens as a model for domestic detention of Guantanamo detainees, saying, "We had 450,000 Japanese and German prisoners housed in the United States during [World War II](32927.md). As a nation, we can deal with this."[^cite_note-186]

### Immigration reform

Graham was a supporter of "comprehensive immigration reform", of [S. 2611](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._2611), the McCain-Kennedy Immigration Reform Bill of 2006, and of [S. 1348](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._1348), the [Comprehensive Immigration Reform Act of 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Immigration_Reform_Act_of_2007). His positions on immigration, and in particular collaborating with Senator [Ted Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Kennedy), earned Graham the ire of conservative activists.[^cite_note-187] The controversy prompted conservative activists to support a primary challenge in 2008 by longtime Republican national committeeman [Buddy Witherspoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddy_Witherspoon),[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189] but Graham won the nomination by a large margin.[^cite_note-190]

In early 2010, Graham began working with Democratic New York senator [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) on immigration reform.[^cite_note-191] Their talks on the matter broke down later that year.[^cite_note-NYDN-2012-192]

In July 2010, Graham suggested that U.S. citizenship as a birthright guaranteed by the [14th Amendment of the U.S. Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/14th_Amendment_of_the_U.S._Constitution) should be amended, and that any children born to illegal immigrants in the United States should be considered illegal immigrants.[^cite_note-193] He alleged, "Half the children born in hospitals on our borders are the children of illegal immigrants."[^cite_note-194]

In November 2012, Graham and Schumer resumed their talks on comprehensive immigration reform.[^cite_note-NYDN-2012-192] On January 28, 2013, Graham was a member of a bipartisan [group of eight senators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gang_of_Eight_\(immigration\)) that announced principles for [comprehensive immigration reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_Security,_Economic_Opportunity,_and_Immigration_Modernization_Act_of_2013).[^cite_note-195] On June 23, 2013, Graham said that the Senate was close to obtaining 70 votes to pass the reform package.[^cite_note-196]

In May 2019, Graham proposed instituting new immigration laws that would only allow migrants to apply for [asylum](1621432.md) from their home country or Mexico, smooth the process to deport unaccompanied children to [Central America](6121.md), and extend the period by which migrant children could be detained from 20 days to 100 days.[^cite_note-197]

In July 2019, Graham visited a migrant detention center in Texas. He reacted that it was not "a concentration camp" but "a facility overwhelmed". Of the [migrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migrant_worker), Graham said, "I don't care if they have to stay in these facilities for 400 days. We're not going to let those men go that I saw. It would be dangerous."[^cite_note-198]

### Internet and technology

In May 2018, Graham voted against legislation that would have overturned an [FCC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FCC) ruling and restored [net neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_neutrality).[^cite_note-199]

In March 2017, Graham voted for the [Broadband Consumer Privacy Proposal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Broadband_Consumer_Privacy_Proposal_repeal) that removed the FCC's internet privacy rules and allowed [internet service providers](100245.md) to sell customers' browsing history without their permission.[^cite_note-200]

In February 2022, Graham and [Richard Blumenthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Blumenthal) introduced bipartisan legislation, as part of the [EARN IT Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EARN_IT_Act), to incentivize tech companies to remove [child sexual abuse material](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_sexual_abuse_material) (CSAM) from their platforms and remove blanket immunity for violations of laws related to online child pornography.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202]

### Gun rights

Graham opposed extending background checks.[^cite_note-203] He said, "universal background checks are going to require universal \[gun] registration."[^cite_note-204] He called current gun laws "broken", citing an example of a woman who pleaded guilty by reason of insanity to attempting to kill President [George W. Bush](3414021.md), but was later able to pass a background check and buy a gun.[^cite_note-205] To this end, in March 2013, he joined senators [Jeff Flake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Flake), [Mark Begich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Begich), and [Mark Pryor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Pryor) in introducing a bill that would close a loophole by flagging people who attempt to buy guns who have used an insanity defense, were ruled dangerous by a court or had been committed by a court to mental health treatment. It did not address the [gun show loophole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_show_loophole).[^cite_note-206]

In 2022, Graham became one of ten Republican senators to support a bipartisan agreement on gun control, which included a red flag provision, a support for state crisis intervention orders, funding for school safety resources, stronger background checks for buyers under the age of 21, and penalties for straw purchases.[^cite_note-207]

### Healthcare

Graham opposed President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama)'s health reform legislation; he voted against the [Affordable Care Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affordable_Care_Act) (Obamacare) in December 2009,[^cite_note-208] and against the [Health Care and Education Reconciliation Act of 2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_Care_and_Education_Reconciliation_Act_of_2010).[^cite_note-209] He played a leading role in efforts to repeal Obamacare, authoring the [Graham–Cassidy amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graham%E2%80%93Cassidy_health_care_amendment) to Republicans' 2017 repeal efforts. The amendment would have given states permission to remove protections for individuals with [preexisting conditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preexisting_conditions), such as allowing insurers to charge them higher prices for insurance.[^cite_note-210]

### Vaccines

Graham criticized Senator [Rand Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rand_Paul) after Paul said, "I've heard of many tragic cases of walking, talking, normal children who wound up with profound mental disorders after vaccines." Graham said that Paul was "creating anxiety for no good reason" and "looking at this issue through a libertarian's eyes, not a physician's eyes".[^cite_note-211]

### Abortion

In 2015, Graham sponsored the Pain-Capable Unborn Child Protection Act in the Senate, which bans [abortion](765.md) after 20 weeks of [gestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gestation) on a national basis, with some exceptions (to save the life of the mother, or when the pregnancy is the result of [rape](7980471.md) or [incest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incest)).[^cite_note-212] In 2018, Graham was [anti-abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-abortion), but said that *[Roe v. Wade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roe_v._Wade)* is precedent that should not be overturned without good reason.[^cite_note-213] In 2020, he was one of 13 Republican senators who declined to sign an amicus brief asking the Supreme Court to overturn *Roe*.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215]

In May 2022, Graham advocated that the Supreme Court overturn *Roe* to ensure that "every state will decide if abortion is legal and on what terms", as this would be "the most constitutionally sound way of dealing with this issue and the way the United States handled the issue until 1973."[^cite_note-216] In June 2022, he asserted that all conservatives "believed that there's nothing in the Constitution giving the federal government the right to regulate abortion".[^cite_note-217] In August 2022, Graham said that "states should decide the issue of abortion" and that he had "been consistent" on this.[^cite_note-Saric-2022-218][^cite_note-219]

After previously saying that abortion should be left up to the states, in September 2022 Graham introduced legislation to institute a federal ban on abortion after 15 weeks of pregnancy with exceptions for rape, incest, and the life of the patient.[^cite_note-220] He said: "This is not a [states' rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States'_rights) issue. This is a human right issue \[...] I am going to advocate a national minimum standard."[^cite_note-221] His proposed legislation would force states to ban abortion after 15 weeks, but it would not require states to allow it up to that point.[^cite_note-222]

### Animal welfare

Graham received high marks from national animal-protection organizations. In the first session of the 119th Congress, the [Humane World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humane_World_for_Animals) Action Fund awarded him a score of 100 on its 2025 Humane Scorecard and designated him a "leader",[^cite_note-223] which indicates participation in actions such as leading "on multiple legislative and/or regulatory efforts" the Humane World Action Fund supports.[^cite_note-224]

Graham was a prominent Republican supporter of efforts to end [horse slaughter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horse_slaughter). In 2025, he co-sponsored the bipartisan Save America's Forgotten Equines (SAFE) Act, which would prohibit commercial slaughter of horses for human consumption and end the export of American horses to foreign slaughter facilities.[^cite_note-225]

### LGBTQ+ rights

In 1996, Graham voted for the [Defense of Marriage Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_of_Marriage_Act),[^cite_note-226] which became federal law that year; it defined marriage as between one man and one woman, and enacted non-recognition of [same-sex marriages](92656.md) at the federal level.[^cite_note-227] Graham reiterated his support of the Defense of Marriage Act in 2022.[^cite_note-228]

Graham voted to support [Federal Marriage Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Marriage_Amendment) in 2006, which would have defined [marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marriage_in_the_United_States) as a union of one man and one woman, as well as prevented judicial extension of marriage rights to [same-sex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_couple) couples. He said, "I believe in the traditional definition of marriage as being between one man and one woman. Traditional marriage is an institution worth protecting and this amendment will accomplish that goal. A constitutional amendment is the only effective way to cut off the growing trend among judges to create a constitutional right to same-sex marriage."[^cite_note-229] After the Supreme Court ruling in *[Obergefell v. Hodges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obergefell_v._Hodges)*, Graham said that although he disagreed with the ruling, he no longer believed that a constitutional amendment was a viable action on the issue.[^cite_note-230]

In November 2022, Graham voted against the [Respect for Marriage Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respect_for_Marriage_Act), which provides federal recognition for [same-sex marriages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_United_States) in the United States. Graham said in August that, "states should decide the issue of marriage \[...] if you're going to ask me to have the federal government take over defining marriage, I'm going to say no".[^cite_note-Saric-2022-218]

### Climate change

On December 10, 2009, Graham and senators [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) and [Joe Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Lieberman) co-sponsored a letter to President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) announcing their commitment to passing a [climate change](5042951.md) bill and outlining its framework.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232] Graham was identified as a potential Republican supporter of a climate change bill and thought to be a likely sponsor of the final bill,[^cite_note-233] but he pulled his support, saying that he disapproved of Senate Democrats moving forward with legislation to deal with immigration issues, a reaction to [Arizona's passage of an illegal immigration law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arizona_SB1070). Graham's withdrawal of support left passage of the climate change bill in doubt.[^cite_note-Broder-2010-234]

In June 2010, Graham told reporters, "The science about global warming has changed. I think they've oversold this stuff, quite frankly. I think they've been alarmist and the science is in question. The whole movement has taken a giant step backward."[^cite_note-235] He also said that he planned to vote against the climate bill he had originally co-sponsored, citing further restriction of [offshore drilling](9969688.md) added to the bill and its impact on transportation.[^cite_note-Broder-2010-234] In 2015, Graham said he "completely understand\[s] and accept\[s]" that climate change is real, but said "I don't know" the role human activity played.[^cite_note-236]

In 2020, Graham sponsored the Growing Climate Solutions Act, a bill that would make it simpler for farmers to sell [carbon credits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_credits) on existing carbon trading markets in California and in the Northeast.[^cite_note-237]

In November 2023, Graham and [Bill Cassidy](9386382.md) co-sponsored the [Foreign Pollution Fee Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Pollution_Fee_Act). Endorsed by the [Sierra Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Club), the bill (S. 3198; referred to the [Senate Finance Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Finance_Committee)) proposed imposing a [carbon tariff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eco-tariff) on energy and industrial [imports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Import) based on the good's [emission intensity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emission_intensity) or [carbon footprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_footprint) as compared with the same domestic good to impose a [carbon price](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_price) on goods from [countries with greater greenhouse gas emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_greenhouse_gas_emissions) than the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions_by_the_United_States).[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240]

### Foreign policy

Graham supported an [interventionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interventionism_\(politics\)) foreign policy.[^cite_note-241] In 2002, he voted for the [Iraq Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Resolution), which authorized military action against [Iraq](7515928.md).[^cite_note-242] He also supported the invasion of Iraq.[^cite_note-243] Graham and senators [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) and [Joe Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Lieberman), who were frequently dubbed "the three amigos",[^cite_note-Kim-2026-3][^cite_note-Collins-2026-25] traveled widely, pushing for United States military intervention, particularly after the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md). Their influence reached its zenith in 2007 as President George W. Bush advocated for his [surge strategy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_War_troop_surge_of_2007) in Iraq and then diminished shortly before Lieberman retired from the Senate in 2013.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245] [Kelly Ayotte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelly_Ayotte), who joined the Senate in 2011, was considered Lieberman's replacement in the group.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247]

Graham was a frequent critic of the [foreign policy of the Barack Obama administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_Barack_Obama_administration). He threatened to derail the confirmation of Obama's nominee for [United States secretary of defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_secretary_of_defense), Republican former senator [Chuck Hagel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Hagel), a centrist.[^cite_note-248] Graham remarked that Hagel "would be the most antagonistic secretary of defense towards the state of Israel in our nation's history."[^cite_note-249]

On February 28, 2013, Graham criticized Obama and both political parties on the Senate floor for allowing the [budget reduction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sequester_\(2013\)) to occur with "two-thirds of the budget" exempt from reductions and said the impact on the [Department of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Defense) would create a "hollow military" that "invites aggression".[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253]

#### War in Afghanistan

Main article: [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](19666611.md)



Graham with [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) and Lt. Gen. [Richard P. Mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_P._Mills_\(general\)) in [Afghanistan](737.md) in 2010

Graham suggested that the U.S. should stay in Afghanistan permanently, claiming that this would benefit both nations, as the U.S. would maintain a clear picture of what was happening in the region, and [Afghan security forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan_security_forces) would have a military advantage that would ensure Afghanistan never fell back into the hands of the [Taliban](30635.md).[^cite_note-254] He further claimed that [Afghan leaders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Afghanistan) accepted this long-term U.S. military presence since it benefited them, but that Iran and some of its allies opposed it, which was considered a debatable claim.[^cite_note-255]

| External videos |
| --- |
| video icon ["Senator Graham on President Biden's Afghanistan Strategy"](https://www.c-span.org/program/news-conference/senator-graham-on-president-bidens-afghanistan-strategy/592250) on [C-SPAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-SPAN) |

Graham vehemently opposed President [Joe Biden](145422.md)'s decision to [withdraw all U.S. troops from Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Withdrawal_of_United_States_troops_from_Afghanistan_\(2020%E2%80%932021\)), originally agreed to by the [first Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Trump_administration).[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257] He predicted that the decision would put the U.S. in danger and could cause "another 9/11".[^cite_note-258] Soon after the withdrawal started, the Taliban launched an [offensive against the Afghan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Taliban_offensive), quickly advancing in front of the collapsing [Afghan Armed Forces](57836785.md).[^cite_note-259] On July 8, 2021, Graham called Biden's decision a "disaster in the making".[^cite_note-260]

#### Iran

On November 6, 2010, Graham called for a preemptive military strike to weaken the government of Iran.[^cite_note-261] In 2011, he supported a continuing U.S. military presence in Iraq, saying, "If we're not smart enough to work with the Iraqis to have 10,000 to 15,000 American troops in Iraq in 2012, Iraq could go to hell."[^cite_note-262]

On an episode of *[Fox and Friends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_and_Friends)*, Graham joked that it would be "terrible" if a DNA test showed he had Iranian ancestry. Co-host [Brian Kilmeade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Kilmeade) responded, "Well, they have great people, just bad leaders," and Graham agreed.[^cite_note-Esfandiari-2018-263][^cite_note-Anapol-2018-264] The president of the [National Iranian American Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_American_Council)[^cite_note-Anapol-2018-264] and a number of high-profile [Iranian-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian-Americans) criticized Graham's comments. Graham has never apologized for his comments.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-Esfandiari-2018-263]

During the [Gaza war](74998519.md), Graham called for the U.S. to threaten [Iran's oil infrastructure](28205267.md) if the conflict escalated.[^cite_note-266] In a [CNN](62028.md) interview, he called for the U.S. and Israel to bomb Iran even if it was not involved in [Hamas](13913.md)'s [attack on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Al-Aqsa_Flood). He also said the U.S. would win a war with Iran if it broke out.[^cite_note-267]

In January 2026, during the [2025–2026 Iranian protests](81976180.md), Graham expressed support and wore a "Make Iran Great Again" hat.[^cite_note-268] The same month, he compared Iran's [Supreme Leader](36814344.md), Ayatollah [Ali Khamenei](385653.md), to [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md), and urged Trump to kill him.[^cite_note-269] He supported the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md), claiming that he would support Israel "until my dying day" and that he would convince South Carolinians to send their children to the Middle East for war against Iran.[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271] Because of [NATO](21133.md)'s refusal to support the United States and Israel against Iran, Graham said he was disgruntled about the alliance and that he would "never look at Europe the same".[^cite_note-272]

After the [2026 Iran war ceasefire](82888027.md) was extended, Graham praised the decision to leave the [naval blockade](293054.md) in place, saying it would put pressure on the Iranian government. He also set out strict conditions for any potential agreement with Iran.[^cite_note-273]

#### Russia

In December 2010, Graham was one of 26 senators to vote against the ratification of [New START](26709878.md),[^cite_note-274] a [nuclear arms reduction](22165.md) treaty between the U.S. and the [Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Federation) obliging both countries to have no more than 1,550 strategic [warheads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warhead) or 700 launchers deployed during the next seven years along with providing a continuation of on-site inspections that halted when [START I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/START_I) expired the previous year. It was the first arms treaty with Russia in eight years.[^cite_note-275]

In August 2011, Graham co-sponsored a resolution supporting [Georgia](48768.md), writing in a statement, "Russia's invasion of Georgian land in 2008 was an act of aggression, not only to Georgia but to all new democracies."[^cite_note-276]

On July 16, 2013, Graham suggested the United States should consider boycotting the [2014 Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Winter_Olympics) in [Sochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sochi), Russia, because of "what the Russian government is doing throughout the world".[^cite_note-277]

#### Ukraine

Graham met President [Leonid Kuchma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonid_Kuchma) at his [dacha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dacha) in [Crimea](163045.md) in 2004. It was his first time visiting Ukraine. Senator John McCain was also part of the United States delegation.[^cite_note-278]

#### Russo-Ukrainian war



Graham with Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md), March 18, 2024

On March 3, 2022, in response to the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), Graham [tweeted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tweeted), "The only way this ends is for somebody in Russia to take this guy out", referring to Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md). The tweet, which was viewed as suggesting that a Russian resident should assassinate Putin, drew backlash from United States politicians, who condemned the idea and proposed heavier sanctions instead.[^cite_note-279]

On May 26, 2023, the [Office of the President of Ukraine](19362024.md) released an edited video showing Graham talking to Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) and remarking that "the Russians are dying", followed by a comment that the [American military assistance to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) was the "best money we've ever spent".[^cite_note-AP-2023-280][^cite_note-281] In response, Graham was sharply criticized by the deputy chairman of the [Security Council of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_Council_of_Russia), [Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev). The Office of the president of Ukraine later released the unedited version of the interview, clarifying that Graham's two remarks were unrelated.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283]

On May 29, 2023, the [Russian Interior Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Interior_Ministry) issued an arrest warrant for Graham for his comments about the war. In response, Graham tweeted that the news brought him "immense joy" and that he would "wear the arrest warrant issued by Putin's corrupt and immoral government as a Badge of Honor".[^cite_note-AP-2023-280] In a follow-up tweet, Graham added that he would submit to the jurisdiction of the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) should Russia attempt to serve the warrant.[^cite_note-284]

On February 13, 2024, Graham voted against the Ukraine, Israel, and Taiwan appropriations bill because it included an effort by senators [James Lankford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Lankford), [Kyrsten Sinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyrsten_Sinema), and [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) to control the [Mexico–United States border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border).[^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286] In April, the House returned the appropriations as four separate bills.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] Graham [joined 79 colleagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_federal_budget#Senate_vote) and the majority of his caucus in passing the appropriations, which Schumer packaged as one bill.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291]

In 2025, Graham authored and championed the [Sanctioning Russia Act](79879253.md), legislation that would impose [secondary sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_sanctions) on Russia, including 500% [tariffs](78448911.md) on countries that buy [Russian oil](10450931.md), [natural gas](42827258.md), [uranium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Russia), and other exports.[^cite_note-292]

#### India

In late August 2025, Graham claimed that the Trump administration's 50% [tariff](55551.md) on Indian imports over Russian oil purchases played a key role in bringing Putin to the negotiating table, culminating in the [2025 Russia–United States summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Russia%E2%80%93United_States_summit). He said that targeting Russia's customers would force them to choose the U.S. economy over Russian energy and argued that losing major buyers like India would pressure Russia economically, framing the tariff as a strategic move. Economist [Jeffrey Sachs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Sachs) sharply criticized Graham, calling him "the worst senator in the U.S." and a "fool", arguing that the tariffs had the opposite effect—unifying [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) countries and undermining U.S. strategic interests.[^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294]

#### Libya

Graham supported the NATO-led [military intervention in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_intervention_in_Libya).[^cite_note-295] On January 29, 2013, Graham said that Secretary of State [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton) "got away with murder" after her testimony about the [2012 Benghazi attack](37018641.md),[^cite_note-296] but the next year he said that the [House Intelligence Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Intelligence_Committee) report on Benghazi was "full of crap" and that the Obama administration had been cleared of many of the charges therein.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299]

#### Israel/Palestine

|  | This section **may contain [excessive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Manual_of_Style/Lists#List_size) or [irrelevant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Neutral_point_of_view#Due_and_undue_weight) examples**. Please help [improve it](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lindsey_Graham\&action=edit) by removing [less pertinent examples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Example_cruft) and [elaborating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:PROSE) on existing ones. *(July 2026)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |



Graham with Israeli prime minister [Benyamin Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benyamin_Netanyahu) on March 15, 2019

On January 5, 2017, Graham condemned Obama for abstaining from [UN Security Council Resolution 2334](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2334), which condemned [Israeli settlement building](15123.md) in the [West Bank](33209.md) and [eastern Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Jerusalem) as a violation of international law.[^cite_note-300]

On March 11, 2019, Graham said he would encourage the Trump administration to recognize the [Golan Heights](7515865.md), which is internationally recognized as part of [Syria](7515849.md), as part of Israel.[^cite_note-301]

After the [2021 Israel–Palestine crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_crisis), Graham visited Israel to meet [Israeli prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_prime_minister) [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) to secure an additional \$1 billion of military aid to the country.[^cite_note-302]

On October 10, 2023, Graham tweeted that he "unapologetically stand\[s] with Israel" during the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-303] He called the conflict a "religious war" and said that [Gaza](12047.md) should be "flattened".[^cite_note-304] On October 31, 2023, Graham said that no amount of [civilian casualties in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_2023_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war) would lead him to question Israel's goal of eradicating [Hamas](13913.md).[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306]

On March 9, 2024, Graham said, "For decades now, Palestinian children have been taught through [UNRWA](32270.md) and other agencies to kill all the Jews" and "somebody needs to pull the Palestinian school system up by its roots and destroy it."[^cite_note-307]

On May 1, 2024, Graham participated in a conference call between U.S. senators and [Karim Ahmad Khan](11606957.md), the [Prosecutor of the International Criminal Court](33903993.md). Khan told investigators that when discussing potential [arrest warrants for Israeli leaders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_warrants_for_Israeli_leaders), Graham said: "You may as well shoot the [hostages](75025451.md) yourself. This court is for Africans." A spokesperson for Graham denied that he said the latter sentence, claiming instead that he said the court's [jurisdiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurisdiction) extends only to places where the [rule of law](25166191.md) has collapsed.[^cite_note-308]

On May 8, 2024, Graham warned the Pentagon against halting arms supply to Israel during the Gaza war, saying, "Give Israel what they need to fight the war they can't afford to lose. This is [Hiroshima and Nagasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki) on steroids."[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] This statement caused a debate in the Japanese [National Diet](163847.md). [Taku Yamazoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taku_Yamazoe) and [Jin Matsubara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin_Matsubara) protested to Foreign Minister [Yōko Kamikawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y%C5%8Dko_Kamikawa).[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-Okuhara-2024-312] Matsubara said, "The US is Japan's greatest ally, but the Japanese should be firm with the US on lines that they cannot cross."[^cite_note-Okuhara-2024-312] Kamikawa told the parliamentary committee, "The atomic bombings took many precious lives and forced people to face unspeakable hardships such as illnesses and disabilities, creating a situation that is extremely regrettable from a humanitarian point of view." She emphasized that the use of nuclear weapons is "not consistent with the spirit of humanitarianism because of their tremendously destructive and lethal power."[^cite_note-313] On May 14, Kamikawa called Graham's remarks "extremely regrettable".[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] [Nihon Hidankyō](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nihon_Hidanky%C5%8D) sent a letter of protest to the U.S. Embassy in Japan that said: "This statement is also in violation of international humanitarian law. With the Nuclear Weapons Convention now in effect, it can only be called an anachronistic and malicious delusion".[^cite_note-316]

On May 10, Graham said that he trusts Israel more than he does [U.S. Secretary of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secretary_of_Defense) [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin).[^cite_note-317]

On May 11, Graham and 11 other Republican senators introduced a resolution condemning the [Biden administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_administration)'s actions to withhold or restrict ammunition and weapons to Israel.[^cite_note-318]

In a May 12 interview, Graham again defended bombing Hiroshima and Nagasaki to end World War II and said, "Give Israel the bombs they need to end the war. They can't afford to lose."[^cite_note-319] This statement attracted international attention. *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)*, [Al Jazeera](30204052.md), and *[India Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India_Today)* reported that Graham suggested that a nuclear strike on Gaza should be carried out.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323] The *[South China Morning Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Morning_Post)* columnist Alex Lo wrote that the American ruling elite is becoming unhinged, bringing up one American genocide after another to justify the Israeli genocide.[^cite_note-324] Hamas issued a statement on the remarks referring to Graham's "deep moral decline and the genocide and colonialism mentality that he harbors" and calling him "among the U.S. political elite, who align themselves with a full-fledged genocidal crime committed by the amoral Israeli army against isolated civilians".[^cite_note-325] [Iranian Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Foreign_Ministry) spokesman [Nasser Kanaani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Kanaani) said, "These horrible statements by an American senator in justifying and encouraging the use of nuclear bombs by the Zionist regime reflect the brutality of those who advocate war and disregard human rights and international resolutions."[^cite_note-326]

On May 20, in response to ICC Chief Prosecutor Karim Khan's announcement of his intention to seek arrest warrants of senior Israeli officials in connection to their alleged responsibility in the [war crimes in the Gaza war](75113830.md), Graham called for sanctions to be placed on the ICC[^cite_note-327] and said: "This outrageous decision is truly a slap in the face to the independent judiciary of Israel, which is renowned for their independence."[^cite_note-328] He added, "If they do this to Israel, we're next".[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330]

On May 29, Graham met with Netanyahu and called [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ) President [Nawaf Salam](21077090.md) a "raving antisemite".[^cite_note-Berman-2024-331][^cite_note-332] Graham visited Israel five times after the Hamas attack on October 7, and Netanyahu said he had no better friend for Israel than Graham.[^cite_note-Berman-2024-331]

On July 5, Graham said, "The Palestinians in Gaza are the most radicalized population on the planet, who are taught to hate Jews from birth".[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335] [CAIR](159138.md) issued a statement condemning his remark.[^cite_note-336]

On November 23, Graham argued that, in response to the [ICC](14880.md) arrest warrants for [Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netanyahu) and [Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant), allied countries such as Canada, the UK, Germany, and France should be [sanctioned](411315.md) if they assist the ICC.[^cite_note-337]

On June 1, 2025, in response to [Greta Thunberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greta_Thunberg) traveling on a ship bringing humanitarian aid to [Gaza](12047.md), Graham tweeted: "Hope Greta and her friends can swim!"[^cite_note-338] At an August 2025 [South Carolina Republican Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Carolina_Republican_Party) meeting, he said, "if America pulls the plug on Israel, God will pull the plug on us".[^cite_note-339]

In 2026, Graham coached Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) on how to convince President Trump to begin [bombing Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_War).[^cite_note-340]

#### Venezuela

In May 2019, Graham called for a military invasion of [Venezuela](32374.md) to overthrow [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) amid the [2019 Venezuelan presidential crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Venezuelan_presidential_crisis).[^cite_note-341]

#### Niger

In October 2017, in the wake of the [Tongo Tongo ambush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tongo_Tongo_ambush), which killed four United States soldiers, Graham said, "I didn't know there was a thousand troops in Niger."[^cite_note-342] A few days later, he called for an expanded role of the United States military in Niger: "You're going to see more actions in Africa, not less; you're going to see more aggression by the United States toward our enemies, not less; you're going to have decisions being made not in the White House but out in the field."[^cite_note-343]

#### Syria

In July 2018, Graham and Senator [Jeanne Shaheen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeanne_Shaheen) visited [Manbij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manbij) in Syria and met the [Manbij Military Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manbij_Military_Council), which led an [offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manbij_offensive_\(2016\)) to liberate the city from [ISIS](9087364.md) in 2016 with help from the [United States-led coalition](44618350.md).[^cite_note-344]

In January 2026, just months after the [Caesar Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesar_Act) had been revoked, Graham repeatedly vowed to introduce the Save the Kurds Act[^cite_note-345] and reimpose "crippling sanctions" on the [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md) and its allies after its [offensive](82106178.md) against [Kurdish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurd) areas in [Rojava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rojava_Kurdistan), in order to "prevent a bloodbath \[...] against \[the United States'] Kurdish allies".[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348] In July 2026, Graham met with Syrian president [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md), supporting him for his role in curbing Iranian influence in his country and urging more positive reassessments of Syria.[^cite_note-349]

#### Saudi Arabia

In March 2015, Graham supported the [Saudi Arabian-led intervention in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabian-led_intervention_in_Yemen),[^cite_note-350] saying, "We want to have a relationship with [Saudi Arabia](349303.md). They're a strategic partner. They're a mortal enemy of the Iranians."[^cite_note-351] In June 2019, he was one of seven Republicans to vote to block Trump's [Saudi arms deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_United_States%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_arms_deal) providing weapons to Saudi Arabia, the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) and [Jordan](7515964.md), and one of five Republicans to vote against an additional 20 arms sales.[^cite_note-352] In late 2019, Graham took a warmer approach toward Saudi Arabia. He praised the Trump administration for sending thousands of additional troops to Saudi Arabia to counter Iran's threat.[^cite_note-353] He also praised Saudi Arabia for opening its airspace to Israeli flights.[^cite_note-354]

#### Turkey

In October 2019, Graham said he would "introduce bipartisan sanctions against Turkey if they invade Syria" and that he would "call for their suspension from NATO if they attack Kurdish forces who assisted the United States in the destruction of the [ISIS Caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISIS_Caliphate)."[^cite_note-355]

#### Cuba

During the [2026 Cuban crisis](82348646.md), Graham said that Cuba's leadership was increasingly vulnerable and that "this communist dictatorship in Cuba \[...] their days are numbered".[^cite_note-356]

#### Greenland

In 2026, Graham expressed support for the [annexation of Greenland](82044223.md) and criticized Europeans as weak.[^cite_note-357] He later expressed indifference to Europeans' fears about Trump's threats to annex Greenland.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359]

#### Armenian genocide

In November 2019, Graham blocked a Senate resolution to officially recognize the [Armenian genocide](140376.md).[^cite_note-360] In December 2019, he voted for the resolution, which passed the Senate unanimously.[^cite_note-361]

### Robert Mueller's investigation

Main articles: [Russian interference in the 2016 United States elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_interference_in_the_2016_United_States_elections) and [Mueller special counsel investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mueller_special_counsel_investigation)

In January 2018, and in the first known congressional [criminal referral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_referral) in the investigation into [Russian interference in the 2016 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_interference_in_the_2016_election), Graham and [Chuck Grassley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Grassley) recommended charges against ex-[MI6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MI6) officer [Christopher Steele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Steele), named as author of the [Steele dossier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steele_dossier).[^cite_note-Fandos-2018-362] Grassley and Graham said that they had reason to believe that Steele had lied to federal authorities.[^cite_note-Fandos-2018-362] According to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, "It was not clear why, if a crime is apparent in the [F.B.I.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F.B.I.) reports that were reviewed by the Judiciary Committee, the Justice Department had not moved to charge Mr. Steele already. The circumstances under which Mr. Steele is alleged to have lied were unclear, as much of the referral was classified."[^cite_note-Fandos-2018-362]

In April 2018, after the FBI raid on the hotel room and offices of Trump's personal attorney, [Michael Cohen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Cohen_\(lawyer\)), Graham, [Cory Booker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cory_Booker), [Chris Coons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Coons), and [Thom Tillis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thom_Tillis) introduced new legislation to "limit President Trump's ability to fire [special counsel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_counsel) [Robert Mueller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Mueller)." Termed the [Special Counsel Independence and Integrity Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Counsel_Independence_and_Integrity_Act), the legislation would allow any special counsel, in this case Mueller, receive an "expedited judicial review" in the 10 days after being dismissed to determine whether the dismissal was appropriate. If not, the special counsel would be reinstated. At the same time, according to *[The Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hill_\(newspaper\))*, the bill would "codify regulations" that a special counsel could only be fired by a senior Justice Department official, who would be required to provide reasons in writing.[^cite_note-363]

On March 14, 2019, Graham blocked a resolution calling for [Mueller's report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mueller's_report) to be made public after it passed the House unanimously.[^cite_note-364]

After Mueller's testimony to two [congressional committees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_committees) on July 24, 2019, Graham speculated that "the Mueller report is in name only. It clearly wasn't the Mueller report. It was just in name."[^cite_note-365] On June 25, 2019, Graham said, "The president gave 1.4 million documents to Mueller. [\[Don\] McGahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_McGahn), his lawyer, testified for 30 hours. He made everybody available to Mueller that Mueller wanted to talk to, and he... answered questions in writing, so this president did nothing to stop Mueller from finding the truth."[^cite_note-366]

### Taxation

Although Graham signed [Grover Norquist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grover_Norquist)'s [Taxpayer Protection Pledge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxpayer_Protection_Pledge) in June 2012, he went on record supporting the closure of tax loopholes without compensating decreases in other tax revenue, saying, "We're so far in debt that if you don't give up some ideological ground, the country sinks."[^cite_note-367]

### Trade

The [Cato Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cato_Institute)'s Center for Trade Policy Studies identifies Graham, during his U.S. House and U.S. Senate tenure, as having had a mostly [protectionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionist) and pro-[subsidies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsidies) voting record.[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369]

### 2015 Charleston church shooting and Confederate flag issue

After [a mass shooting at a historic African American church in Charleston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charleston_church_shooting) on June 17, 2015, Graham canceled all campaign events to return to South Carolina. In response to questions from the press regarding the calls from some to remove the [Confederate flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_flag) at a war memorial on the [South Carolina State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Carolina_State_Capitol) grounds, Graham said, "Well, at the end of the day it's time for people in South Carolina to revisit that decision. \[That] would be fine with me, but this is part of who we are." He continued, "The flag represents to some people a [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_War_\(US\)), and that was the symbol of one side. To others it's a [racist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racist) symbol, and it's been used by people—it's been used in a racist way."[^cite_note-370] Of the shooter responsible for the incident, Graham said, "We're not going to give this a guy an excuse about a book he might have read, or a movie he watched, or a song he listened to, or a symbol out anywhere. It's him ... not the flag."[^cite_note-371]

In a statement issued later, Graham said, "There can be no doubt that the shooting ... was racially motivated and signals to all of us that the scars of our history are still with us today. This murderer said he wanted to start a [race war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_war); he has failed miserably. In Charleston this weekend, I saw a community coming together. I saw people seeking solace in what they share together, not in what makes them different."[^cite_note-372]

### Fiscal Responsibility Act of 2023

Graham was among the 31 Senate Republicans who voted against final passage of the [Fiscal Responsibility Act of 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiscal_Responsibility_Act_of_2023).[^cite_note-373]

### Arctic Frost investigation

Main article: [Arctic Frost investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arctic_Frost_investigation)

In October 2025, documents released by Senator [Chuck Grassley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Grassley) showed that Graham was among nine Republican lawmakers whose phone [metadata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metadata) the FBI obtained as part of the [Arctic Frost investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arctic_Frost_investigation) into efforts to overturn the 2020 presidential election.[^cite_note-Reilly-2025-374] The FBI obtained toll records—which include call times, numbers dialed, and duration, but not call content—covering January 4–7, 2021.[^cite_note-Reilly-2025-374] Graham said that both his personal and government phone records were subpoenaed.[^cite_note-Rimmer-2025-375] After legislation passed allowing senators to sue for damages if their phone records were obtained without notification, Graham announced he would pursue legal action against the Department of Justice, former Special Counsel [Jack Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Smith_\(lawyer\)), and [Verizon](18619278.md).[^cite_note-Rimmer-2025-375] He told reporters he intended to seek "far more" than the \$500,000 minimum specified in the law, saying he wanted to "make it so painful no one ever does this again".[^cite_note-376]

## Campaign contributions

According to a 2013 analysis by the [Center for Public Integrity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Public_Integrity), Graham received about \$223,000 in campaign contributions bundled by lobbyists during the 2011–12 election cycle, about 10.3% of his campaign funds. The analysis ranked him as having the second highest proportion of lobbyist-bundled contributions among members of Congress during that period. Graham was not up for reelection until 2014. His bundlers included organizations and [lobbyists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobbyist) whose positions Graham endorsed.[^cite_note-377]

In 2016, *[The Boston Globe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Boston_Globe)* reported that Graham was "the only Republican recipient of money from a major Democratic donor now facing scrutiny for some questionable campaign donation habits."[^cite_note-Kropf-2016-378] The Thornton Law Firm is nationally known for its expertise in asbestos-related litigation. Over a ten-year period, Graham received \$62,800 in [campaign contributions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campaign_contributions) from the firm's partners. *The Boston Globe* found that the firm, in almost every case, would reimburse partners' political contributions—in the exact amount[^cite_note-Kropf-2016-378]—within 10 days of the contributions being made. Between 2010 and 2014, the firm's partners and one of their wives contributed \$1.6 million to politicians; \$1.4 million was given back to the partners from the firm. The firm told reporters that according to outside consultants the practice was not unlawful because the checks are not bonuses, instead coming out of the partners' firm equity accounts.[^cite_note-Kropf-2016-378]

A spokesman for Graham said that Graham would return the money he received from the firm's lawyers if the law firm were indicted or convicted on corruption charges.[^cite_note-Kropf-2016-378]

## 2016 presidential campaign

Main article: [Lindsey Graham 2016 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lindsey_Graham_2016_presidential_campaign)



Secretary of State [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) meets with senators [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain), [John Barrasso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Barrasso) and Lindsey Graham in [Jerusalem](16043.md) on January 3, 2014.

Graham supported [John McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McCain) for president in 2000 and served as national co-chair of McCain's 2008 presidential campaign.[^cite_note-Draper-2010-34][^cite_note-379]

In [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_United_States_presidential_election), Graham's endorsement was highly sought,[^cite_note-380] but he declined to endorse a [Republican candidate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_presidential_candidates,_2012) before the January [South Carolina Republican primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_election_in_South_Carolina,_2012#Republican_primary).[^cite_note-381] After [Rick Santorum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Santorum) withdrew from the race in April 2012, leaving [Mitt Romney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitt_Romney) as the presumptive nominee, Graham endorsed Romney.[^cite_note-382]

During his Senate reelection race in October 2014, while discussing immigration and foreign policy issues with a reporter from *[The Weekly Standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Weekly_Standard)*, Graham said, "If I get through my general election, if nobody steps up in the presidential mix, if nobody's out there talking ... I may just jump in to get to make these arguments."[^cite_note-383] On March 7, 2015, at a "Politics and Pies" forum, Graham advocated the reversal of defense spending cuts and quipped: "If I were President of the United States, I wouldn't let Congress leave town until we fix this. I would literally use the military to keep them in if I had to."[^cite_note-384]

On April 19, 2015, Graham told [Chris Wallace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Wallace), on the *[Fox News Sunday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_News_Sunday)* show, that he was "91% sure" he would run for president. "If I can raise the money, I'll do it," he said.[^cite_note-385] On May 18, 2015, Graham informally announced that he would run for president on *[CBS This Morning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_This_Morning)*, saying he was running because he thinks "the world is falling apart."[^cite_note-386]

Graham announced [his candidacy for President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lindsey_Graham_presidential_campaign,_2016) on June 1, 2015.[^cite_note-Rappeport-2015-387]

Graham [sought the Republican presidential nomination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lindsey_Graham_2016_presidential_campaign) in 2016 but withdrew before the primaries began.[^cite_note-Rappeport-2015-387] On December 21, 2015, Graham suspended his presidential campaign, due to lack of support and poor polling, and on January 15, 2016, endorsed former [Florida governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Governors_of_Florida) [Jeb Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeb_Bush).[^cite_note-388] After it appeared certain that Donald Trump would become the Republican nominee in May 2016, Graham announced that he would not vote for Trump or [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton), commenting: "I think Donald Trump is going to places where very few people have gone and I'm not going with him."[^cite_note-389] On November 8, 2016, Graham announced that he had voted for [Evan McMullin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evan_McMullin).[^cite_note-Wang-2019-91]
