## Personal life

Referred to in a *[Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Post)* headline as a "[confirmed bachelor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confirmed_bachelor)",[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] Graham denied being gay.[^cite_note-396] While never publicly romantically linked to a woman,[^cite_note-397] he wrote in his memoir that he had a girlfriend in law school named Debbie, and two more during his time in the Air Force in Germany: a JAG officer named Carol, who later served on [Colin Powell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Powell)'s staff, and a flight attendant named Sylvia, to whom he considered proposing.[^cite_note-398] He wrote, "I never found time to meet the right girl, or the right girl was smart enough not to have time for me."[^cite_note-399] On the *[I've Had It](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I've_Had_It_\(podcast\))* podcast shortly after Graham's death, [Hunter Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_Biden) noted the friendship between his father [Joe Biden](145422.md) and Graham[^cite_note-400] and said that "of course" Graham was gay.[^cite_note-401]

Graham had residences in Washington, D.C., and [Seneca, South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seneca,_South_Carolina). A [Southern Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Baptist), he was a member of Seneca's Corinth Baptist Church.[^cite_note-bio-17]

## Death



Graham's desk on the Senate floor following his death

Graham died after a "brief and sudden illness" on the evening of July 11, 2026,[^cite_note-Diamond-2026-402][^cite_note-403] a day after returning from an official visit to [Kyiv](585629.md), Ukraine.[^cite_note-Diamond-2026-402] [Emergency medical services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_medical_services) responded to a call at a home owned by Graham at around 8\:30 p.m. [EDT](496028.md) in the [Capitol Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Hill) section of Washington, D.C. for a patient with chest pain. According to radio transmissions, personnel reported 25 minutes later that a male was in [cardiac arrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_arrest) and that [CPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CPR) was under way. On July 12, preliminary findings from the D.C. [medical examiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_examiner) stated that Graham died from an [aortic dissection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aortic_dissection) caused by [arteriosclerotic cardiovascular disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arteriosclerotic_cardiovascular_disease).[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405]

### Reactions

#### Domestic

Upon the news of Graham's death, President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) called him a "true American Patriot" and said he was "like a member of the family".[^cite_note-406] Trump later said he spoke with Graham the night before his death about the [SAVE America Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAVE_America_Act), describing him as a close ally and friend who could work across party lines.[^cite_note-407] Former presidents [Joe Biden](145422.md) and [George W. Bush](3414021.md) also honored Graham's public service. Biden recalled their years working together in the Senate, saying they often disagreed but shared a commitment to public service and respect for the institution.[^cite_note-CNN-2026-408] Bush praised Graham as a knowledgeable senator who understood America's role in the world and was dedicated to his country.[^cite_note-Hubbard-2026-409]

Graham's colleagues in Congress also paid tribute. [U.S. Senate Majority Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Senate_Majority_Leader) [John Thune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Thune) called him a strong advocate for the United States, while fellow South Carolina senator [Tim Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Scott) said America had lost a statesman and he had lost a friend.[^cite_note-410] McMaster called Graham "the fiercest of fighters for South Carolina and America".[^cite_note-Diamond-2026-402] Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md) recalled disagreements with Graham over Ukraine funding but praised him as someone who fought for his beliefs and supported others when it mattered.[^cite_note-Schneid-2026-411] House Speaker [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson) praised Graham's lifelong service and commitment to "American strength and exceptionalism".[^cite_note-Hutchinson-2026-412]

Republican members of South Carolina's congressional delegation also mourned Graham's death. [Nancy Mace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Mace) called him "funny", "smart", and "a force to be reckoned with", while [Joe Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Wilson) called him an "American patriot". [Ralph Norman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ralph_Norman) remembered him as a "son of South Carolina" who deeply loved the state, and [Russell Fry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell_Fry) praised him as an "extraordinary public servant" and consequential leader whose legacy would be measured by the many people he helped.[^cite_note-Hubbard-2026-409]

Democratic senators remembered Graham's bipartisan approach and willingness to work across party lines. Senators [Richard Blumenthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Blumenthal), [Mark Warner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Warner), and [Dick Durbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Durbin) highlighted his work on Ukraine, Russia sanctions, and Senate negotiations.[^cite_note-Hubbard-2026-409] [Amy Klobuchar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Klobuchar) praised his commitment to public service, willingness to tackle difficult issues, and efforts to assist [Afghan refugees](3657017.md). [Elizabeth Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Warren), [Cory Booker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cory_Booker), and [Adam Schiff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Schiff) also recalled his ability to negotiate despite disagreements, citing his bipartisan work on criminal justice reform, national security, and Ukraine.[^cite_note-CNN-2026-408] [Chris Coons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Coons), who spent Graham's 71st birthday with him during the [2026 NATO summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_NATO_summit) in Turkey, called him "no better friend, no tougher adversary" and urged Congress to honor his memory through legislation.[^cite_note-CNN-2026-408] [James Clyburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Clyburn) highlighted their relationship built on mutual respect despite political differences.[^cite_note-Schneid-2026-411] Former vice president [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) remembered Graham as a witty, energetic colleague who cared deeply about the Senate and South Carolina.[^cite_note-Hutchinson-2026-412]

#### International

[President of the European Commission](24468.md) [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) offered her condolences to Graham's family and called him "a determined and fearless leader".[^cite_note-413] Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) released a statement offering his condolences, saying: "America and the world have lost a resolute leader."[^cite_note-414] [Mark Rutte](4321056.md), [secretary general of NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_general_of_NATO), said Graham was "a powerful advocate for America"; German chancellor [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md) said he was "a true friend and partner of Germany in the transatlantic alliance".[^cite_note-415] Finnish president [Alexander Stubb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Stubb) mourned Graham as a "personal friend", praising his commitment to NATO, transatlantic cooperation, and support for Ukraine.[^cite_note-Schneid-2026-411] Estonian foreign minister [Margus Tsahkna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margus_Tsahkna), Latvian foreign minister [Baiba Braže](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baiba_Bra%C5%BEe), and Lithuanian foreign minister [Kęstutis Budrys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C4%99stutis_Budrys) jointly paid tribute to Graham, remembering him as a steadfast friend and a strong supporter of NATO, transatlantic relations, and Ukraine.[^cite_note-AP-2026-416] Canadian prime minister [Mark Carney](13571049.md) said Graham "stood resolutely in defence of democracy and freedom".[^cite_note-417]

[Prime Minister of Israel](24898.md) [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) said: "Israel has lost one of its greatest friends. America has lost a great patriot. I have lost a beloved friend." He offered his condolences to Graham's family and the American people.[^cite_note-418] Netanyahu later recalled one of his final conversations with Graham, saying that Graham had strongly opposed any reduction of [U.S. military aid to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._military_aid_to_Israel) and viewed support for Israel's security as connected to U.S. interests.[^cite_note-419] Israeli president [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) also paid tribute to Graham, calling him a "great American patriot" a "great friend of Israel", and a "beacon of moral clarity", and praising his support for the [U.S.–Israel partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States%E2%80%93Israel_relations).[^cite_note-420] Defense Minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) said Graham "stood with Israel in its most difficult moments" and was an unwavering supporter of Israel's security and its alliance with the United States.[^cite_note-421] Former Israeli ambassador [Michael Oren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Oren) said Graham's death was a "diplomatic and strategic loss" for Israel, while Iranian state media called Graham a "warmongering and anti-Iranian" senator.[^cite_note-AP-2026-416] Iranian opposition leader [Reza Pahlavi](613947.md) said: "His support for Iran's Lion and Sun Revolution earned him the title 'Uncle Lindsey' among Iranians. He will be remembered with profound gratitude and deep respect".[^cite_note-422]

Iranian state television aired a segment celebrating Graham's death and congratulating the Iranian people. The clip went viral on many Iranian outlets and social media accounts, which long viewed him as a leading advocate of military action against Tehran. During the [state funeral of Ali Khamenei](82570060.md) a week earlier, demonstrators carried signs with Graham's image alongside other figures as future assassination targets. Iranian social media accounts posted images of his face crossed out with a red X on a graphic representing the perpetrators of the [United States attacks on Iran](82533582.md).[^cite_note-423] [Iranian Foreign Ministry spokesman](51080171.md) [Esmail Baghaei](81031766.md) said of Graham's death, "the Angel of Death is just" and "for such a person, whose entire record is one of malice and support for aggression, nothing but a black record will remain in the minds of people. \[...] the death of this radical senator will certainly not sadden the heart of any free person."[^cite_note-424]

### Memorial services

President Trump ordered flags lowered in observance of Graham's death.[^cite_note-425] Governor Henry McMaster also issued an executive order to have flags lowered in South Carolina.[^cite_note-426]

### Washington, D.C. service

A memorial service for Graham took place on July 28 in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md).[^cite_note-Roll_Call-2026-427]

#### Capitol Rotunda

At an arrival ceremony at the [U.S. Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Capitol), Graham was honored in the Capitol Rotunda. U.S. House Chaplain [Margaret Kibben](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margaret_Kibben) opened the Rotunda. Representative [Sheri Biggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheri_Biggs), Senate Majority Leader [John Thune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Thune), and Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md) gave eulogies, and, after a military hymn, U.S. Senate chaplain [Barry C. Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_C._Black) led closing prayer. Darline Graham and Senate Minority Leader [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) were among those in attendance.[^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431]

#### Washington National Cathedral



President [Donald Trump](4848272.md), Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md), Treasury Secretary [Scott Bessent](38532044.md), businessman [Eric Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Trump), and Justice [Brett Kavanaugh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brett_Kavanaugh) attending Graham's funeral service on July 28, 2026

Later that afternoon, Graham's casket arrived in the [Washington National Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_National_Cathedral), where his funeral was held. [Randy Hollerith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randy_Hollerith), the dean of the Washington National Cathedral, presided over the service, with passages from Scripture also read by [Tony Perkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Perkins), [Marjorie Dannenfelser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Dannenfelser), and [Franklin Graham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Graham).[^cite_note-432] President Trump delivered a eulogy, as did Fox News presenter [Sean Hannity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Hannity), Graham's niece Emilie Boggs Roberts, and Tim Tate, the lead pastor of Corinth Baptist Church in [Seneca, South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seneca,_South_Carolina), who was Graham's personal pastor.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435] Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md), Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md), and Finnish President [Alexander Stubb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Stubb) attended the afternoon service.[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

### Columbia service

On July 29, Graham was honored at the [South Carolina State House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Carolina_State_House) in Columbia, South Carolina, with a flyover as his funeral procession made its way to First Baptist Church. Graham's funeral was open to the public, but his burial service in [Pickens County, South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pickens_County,_South_Carolina), was private. [^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439]

Attendees included Attorney General of South Carolina [Alan Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Wilson_\(politician\)), State Representative [Jermaine Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jermaine_Johnson_\(politician\)), former Ambassador to Canada [David H. Wilkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_H._Wilkins), former Congressman [Henry Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Brown_\(South_Carolina_politician\)),[^cite_note-440] Lieutenant Governor [Pamela Evette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamela_Evette), U.S. Representatives [Ralph Norman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ralph_Norman) and [Sheri Biggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheri_Biggs), former South Carolina Governors [Nikki Haley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikki_Haley) and [Mark Sanford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Sanford), Fox News host [Sean Hannity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sean_Hannity),[^cite_note-441] Treasury Secretary [Scott Bessent](38532044.md), Judge [J. Michelle Childs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Michelle_Childs), South Carolina House Speaker [Murrell Smith Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murrell_Smith_Jr.), State Senator [Karl B. Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_B._Allen), and preservationist [Catherine Fleming Bruce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_Fleming_Bruce).[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443]

Speakers included Governor [Henry McMaster](3482397.md), Congressman [Jim Clyburn](784733.md), Senator [Tim Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Scott), *[Fox & Friends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_&_Friends)* co-host [Ainsley Earhardt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ainsley_Earhardt), and former congressman [Trey Gowdy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trey_Gowdy).[^cite_note-444]

### Central, South Carolina burial

After his funeral procession passed through town, witnessed by an estimated crowd of 5,000, Graham was buried in Mt. Zion Cemetery in Central, South Carolina.[^cite_note-445]

## Legacy and memorials

On August 10, 2026, [Joint Base Charleston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Base_Charleston) was renamed Joint Base Lindsey Graham. Present at the naming ceremony were US Senator [Darline Graham](83683581.md), South Carolina Governor [Henry McMaster](3482397.md), Treasury Secretary [Scott Bessent](38532044.md) and Secretary of Defense [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md).[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447]

On August 23, 2026, the [University of South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_South_Carolina) announced its intention to name its planned new ROTC building the Senator Lindsey Graham ROTC Center of Excellence.[^cite_note-448]

On September 19, 2026, US President Donald Trump signed the "[Lindsey O. Graham Sanctioning Russia and Iran Act of 2026](79879253.md)," which allows him to impose tariffs of up to 100 percent on Russian oil buyers, including China and India, in response to the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](70149799.md), as well as to extend [existing sanctions on Iran](9941859.md).[^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450]
